<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full picture. Let me analyze the Action Matrix against the review report and provide my independent assessment.

The review report has 8 "Questions for Authors" (4 from R1, 4 from R2). Let me also check the review_issues.yaml to see if I missed anything.

From review_issues.yaml, I see these issues:
1. R1-W1 (Critical): β=0.3 single seed, abstract '87%' single seed, eval set 100 examples
2. R1-W2 (Critical): ISR framing - "maximal tightness", "genuine" terminology, ρ=-0.049 buried


3. R2-W1 (Critical): Limitations section format - needs independent \section
4. R2-W2 (Critical): Single model/dataset, title plural implies generality

Then there are several major warnings: the s43 eval protocol mixing sampling and greedy approaches, the "solver-agnostic" claim that's too broad, an unfair SFT baseline comparison, insufficient differentiation from Kim et al., the Meta-Goodhart novelty framing issue, and what appears to be another warning that got cut off.

Moving through the minor issues now: precision and recall details for the H1/H7 classifiers, the justification for the uniform distribution, code and data availability, and some numbering inconsistencies. There's also a critical gap in the references that needs addressing.

The action matrix shows I've mapped most of the critical and major issues to specific review findings, though a few mappings are still pending completion.

Now looking at the first substantive question about C4 — whether limiting the scope in approach A versus B is sufficient, or if I need to add model experiments. Both reviewers flagged this as critical/major, with R2 specifically calling for either additional experiments across different model scales and architectures, or explicit scope limitations in the title and claims.

The upgrade path to a 4/6 score mentions adding significance tests, running with β=0.3 across 3 seeds, and including another logic type like SMT-LIB or Lean, but it doesn't explicitly require a second model. That said, R2's core weakness is the narrow empirical scope — just one 1.5B model with two logic types. Approach A of simply limiting scope is risky given R2's Critical rating; for a findings track it might work, but for the main conference it probably won't be enough.

Adding Qwen3-1.5B is pragmatic but limited — testing another size within the same family shows scale sensitivity rather than architectural generality, which doesn't directly address the diversity the upgrade path seems to want. Still, the rubric doesn't explicitly demand architectural diversity, just model or logic type variation. My best move is combining Approach A with running Qwen3-1.5B on the execution-only subset — that's a reasonable middle ground that demonstrates the ISR collapse pattern holds across scales without requiring a full second evaluation. Going with just Approach A risks rejection because reviewers might see it as avoiding the real problem rather than solving it. 条件实验。

Actually, wait - I should think about this more carefully. The reviewer says "one model size (1.5B)" which is wrong (it's actually Qwen3-4B based on the paper). But the point stands - only one model was tested. Adding Qwen3-1.5B shows the phenomenon persists at different scales. This is actually a reasonable choice because:
1. Same tokenizer/architecture eliminates confounds
2. Shows scale doesn't matter (or does)
3. 4 GPU·h is affordable
4. Different architecture (LLaMA) would require new prompting templates, potentially new bugs, and more GPU time

So Qwen3-1.5B is a reasonable choice for the deadline. Not as strong as LLaMA, but pragmatic and still valuable.

**Q2: β=0.3 补 seed 后是否需要更新 dose-response narrative?**

Yes. The current narrative only has one data point at β=0.3, but adding three seeds changes things. I should report mean±std for β=0.3 like I do for β=0.1, and if the cross-seed variance is high, that reinforces the instability finding. If β=0.3 shows consistent collapse across seeds, the "classic Goodhart" claim at high dose becomes much stronger. I'll need to update Table 1 to show the three-seed means for that row, which directly addresses the "anecdote not trajectory" criticism.

**Q3: M2 SFT baseline — binomial CI 够不够？需要 PR-matched baseline 吗？**

The reviewer's concern about SFT PR... The reviewer didn't explicitly ask for a PR-matched baseline, so I can frame this as a FOLIO-specific discussion point in the rebuttal. I realize we can actually run the H1/H7 classifier on the 100-step checkpoint outputs from plan003 without new experiments—this would let us check whether hacking patterns emerge at PR=32%, which is closer to the SFT baseline's 14%. So my recommendation is to combine the binomial CI with the proposed rewording, plus this supplementary H1/H7 analysis on the early checkpoint.

Now I'm checking the review issues against what we've actually addressed. Most of the 15 issues map to our action items, but I'm noticing that Q1 about the ISR collapse trajectory is only partially covered—the reviewer specifically wants to see the s43/s44 execution-only collapse across multiple checkpoints (100→500→1000), not just single snapshots. And Q4 about inter-annotator agreement for the taxonomy isn't in our plan at all.

Looking at R2's questions, the meta-Goodhart distinction and third logic type are handled, but the ISR plus anti-gaming regularization and Shapley versus LOO comparisons are more discussion points than experiments. The bigger gap is R1-Q4: the reviewer wants to know about our annotation methodology for the 200-output taxonomy—whether it's single-annotator or multi-annotator work, and what the agreement metrics are.

I should clarify that our classification is actually automated using text-matching and functional LOO rather than manual annotation, and we can point to the precision/recall already reported in M1. For the anti-gaming regularization question, that's something to address thoughtfully in the rebuttal as future work rather than a new experiment. The Shapley versus LOO comparison belongs in the Discussion as a limitation. On the ISR side, R1 is asking for the collapse trajectory across all three seeds (s42, s43, s44), not just s42—we have 500-step data for all of them but only 1000-step for s42, so we might need to show all three trajectories in a figure or supplementary text.

Looking at what actually requires new experiments: R1-Q1 might need 1000-step runs for the other seeds unless we can argue they've already fully collapsed by 500 steps; R1-Q2 just needs a statistical test on existing data; R1-Q3 is already covered by the β=0.3 three-seed result; R1-Q4 is just a methodology clarification; R2-Q1 is reframing existing text; R2-Q2 could be proposed as future work or tackled with a quick experiment using a minimum-premise constraint; R2-Q3 is a discussion point; and R2-Q4 depends on whether we're running Qwen3-1.5B.

For the β=0.3 seed experiments, I need to be strict about configuration: use the exact same hyperparameters as the existing s42 runs, test only seeds 43 and 44, keep the training data constant (train_500_seed42.jsonl), evaluate with greedy decoding on the same 100-example held-out set, and run both ISR and Random variants for each seed to get 4 total runs.

The key baseline numbers I'm comparing against are plan002's eval PR of 0.18 (despite training at 0.5525) and plan017's eval PR of 0.48, both trained for 500 steps, so my new runs need to match that step count exactly.
```

I now have all the data needed. Here is the comprehensive summary of the reviewer feedback.

---

The primary consolidated reviewer feedback is in `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, which is the structured "Round 0" review issues file. Three separate dream review rounds were also run (2026-05-13, 2026-05-14 morning, 2026-05-14 evening), each producing best/median/worst tier reports. The consensus scores across all rounds and tiers were **3/6 (Weak Reject)** -- which maps to the "Overall 3/5" you mentioned (different scales).

Below are all reviewer concerns, organized by severity, drawn from the `review_issues.yaml` (the canonical consolidated source) and supplemented with detail from the dream review reports.

---

## CRITICAL (C1-C4)

**C1 / R1-W1: Statistical power severely insufficient**
- β=0.3 tested with only a single seed. Abstract's "87%" comes from single seed (s42). Evaluation set is only 100 examples. The paper itself reveals σ=47pp cross-seed variance yet runs key conditions at n=1, creating an internal contradiction. No significance tests, confidence intervals, or effect sizes reported anywhere. The premise-count-matched comparison rests on n=11 vs n=21 samples.
- Action: β=0.3 must have 3 seeds; abstract single-seed numbers must be labeled; confidence intervals needed throughout.

**C2 / R1-W2: ISR ≠ semantic faithfulness, but framing implies it**
- ISR correlates with NLI accuracy at ρ=-0.049 (essentially null), yet abstract uses "maximal tightness" language implying quality. The term "genuine" carries semantic correctness connotations but is operationally defined by exclusion (not H1-H7). The ISR caveat is buried in §4.2; it should appear in abstract/intro. ISR gives false positives: ISR=0.57 for tautological P→P proofs, ISR=0.83-0.90 for conclusion duplication artifacts.
- Action: Prominently state ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness. Redefine or limit "genuine" terminology.

**C3 / R2-W1: Limitations section format non-compliant**
- Limitations embedded as a `\paragraph` within the Conclusion rather than an independent `\section{Limitations}`. This risks desk rejection at venues requiring a standalone Limitations section (e.g., EMNLP, ACL).
- Action: Create standalone `\section{Limitations}` after Conclusion, before References.

**C4 / R2-W2: Single model + single dataset, but title implies generality**
- All experiments use only Qwen3-4B (or Qwen2.5-1.5B -- model identity itself is inconsistent across documents) and FOLIO dataset. Title uses plural "Neurosymbolic Formalizers" implying generality. Prolog results are thin/incomplete. No SMT-LIB, no Lean, no second model scale with multi-seed validation.
- Action: Add at least one different-scale/architecture model, or explicitly constrain title/claims to the tested configuration.

---

## MAJOR / WARNING (M1-M7)

**M1 / R1-W4: Exec-only seed 43 used sampling (T=0.7) instead of greedy decoding**
- Seed 43 evaluated with temperature sampling rather than eval_sta.py greedy decoding, violating the paper's own C004 constraint. Despite an appendix footnote calling it "not strictly comparable," the result is still included in cross-seed summary statistics.
- Action: Re-evaluate seed 43 with greedy decoding, or remove from cross-seed aggregates with clear explanation.

**M2 / R1-W3: "Solver-agnostic" overclaimed**
- Only 2 solvers tested (Prover9, SWI-Prolog). Method is solver-agnostic in principle, but empirical validation covers only two logic types (FOL and Prolog, which share logical structure). No discussion of applicability to SMT, Lean 4, Coq, or other complex formal systems where component definition and LOO applicability differ fundamentally.
- Action: Narrow claim to "solver-agnostic in principle, validated on two solvers" or add more solver validations.

**M3 / R2-W6: SFT baseline comparison unfair due to pass rate gap**
- SFT has PR=14% vs GRPO PR=93%. Low PR inherently reduces hacking opportunity. "RL-specific hacking" claim may be more accurately "hacking correlates with aggressive optimization." SFT has only 13 genuine samples, giving binomial 95% CI of approximately [66%, 100%].
- Action: Adjust "RL-specific" framing or add a baseline with comparable PR level.

**M4 / R2-W7: Insufficient differentiation from Kim et al. (2026)**
- "Do LLMs Game Formalization?" studies the same phenomenon (formalization gaming) in Lean 4. Only 2 sentences of comparison in related_work.tex. Reviewers want an explicit 2-3 sentence comparison paragraph detailing setup differences and complementarity.
- Action: Add substantive comparison paragraph in Related Work.

**M5 / R1-W5: "Meta-level Goodhart" novelty insufficiently argued**
- The conceptual distinction between "gaming the reward" (standard Goodhart) and "gaming the diagnostic-turned-reward" (meta-Goodhart) is a matter of degree, not kind. The paper itself calls it "a specific instance of adversarial Goodhart." Reviewers suggest reframing as "first empirical demonstration" rather than claiming conceptual novelty.
- Action: Weaken conceptual novelty claim, emphasize empirical demonstration value.

**M6 / R1-W6: Training scale (500 examples / 100 eval) limitations under-discussed**
- train_500_seed42.jsonl suggests seed-42 sampling for training data, possible selection bias. Whether data scale affects hacking mode diversity or ISR collapse speed is not discussed. 100 eval problems gives ±5-10pp binomial CI on pass rate.
- Action: Discuss data scale limitations in Limitations section; explain training subset selection method.

**M7 / R2-MISSING-REF: Reference coverage severely insufficient**
- Only ~33 references (some rounds found as few as 7-8). Critical omissions:
  - PRM literature: ThinkPRM, VersaPRM, R-PRM, Lightman et al. 2023, Math-Shepherd
  - Reward hacking theory: Skalse et al. 2022 (NeurIPS), Gao et al. 2023 (scaling laws for reward overoptimization)
  - Reward shaping: Ng et al. 1999
  - Code generation reward hacking literature
  - Neurosymbolic competitors: Logic-RL, LINC, Logic-LM, SatLM, RLSF
  - Mutation testing: DeMillo 1978 (ISR is essentially a first-order mutation score)
  - Constrained RLHF: Moskovitz et al. 2024
  - Benchmarks: ProofWriter, ProntoQA, Multi-LogiEval
  - "Spurious Rewards" (Yang et al. 2025) -- random reward matching ground-truth in GRPO
- Action: Expand to 25+ references with substantive engagement.

---

## MINOR / INFO (m1-m4)

**m1 / R1-W7: H1/H7 classifier precision/recall unreported**
- Prolog s44 "query-as-fact" pattern is reported as 100% genuine by text-match classifier, suggesting near-zero recall for certain hacking modes. FOL Genuine% may be systematically overestimated.
- Action: Report classifier precision/recall; discuss potential systematic bias.

**m2 / R1-W8: Random control U(0,1) distribution mismatch**
- Random control uses U(0,1) while ISR has a skewed distribution. R2 partially rebuts this as a conservative design choice. Minor impact but should be justified.
- Action: Add justification for U(0,1) choice in §4.3.

**m3 / R1-W9: No code/data release commitment**
- Understandable during double-blind review, but paper should include a commitment to release upon acceptance.
- Action: Add "code and data will be released upon acceptance" statement.

**m4 / R2-W8: H1/H7 numbering incoherent**
- Hacking modes numbered H1, H7 with H2-H6 not existing. Creates confusion.
- Action: Renumber sequentially or use descriptive names throughout.

---

## Additional Recurring Themes from Dream Reviews (not individually numbered in review_issues.yaml)

1. **Coarse beta sweep**: Only β=0.1 and β=0.3 tested. "Dose-response" and "sharp transition" language unsupported by 2 data points. Reviewers want β ∈ {0.05, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.25, 0.3, 0.5}.

2. **Model identity inconsistency**: Paper variously says "Qwen-1.5B", "Qwen3-1.7B", "Qwen3-4B". Must be resolved.

3. **Missing training hyperparameters**: Learning rate, batch size, KL penalty, rollout count, optimizer not reported.

4. **No positive method proposed**: Paper is purely diagnostic/negative. Reviewers consistently note this raises the bar for empirical thoroughness. Even a preliminary positive direction (ISR-guided filtering, adaptive reward gating) would help.

5. **ISR is LOO ablation -- limited methodological novelty**: Every review round flags this. The contribution is application-level, not technique-level. RIP framework invocation is "cosmetic" -- only Propagation is measured.

6. **Data integrity concerns** (in the "best" tier worst-case review): Some tables may present results from experiments not yet completed at draft time. SMT-LIB, 8B scaling, curriculum ablation, and multi-logic joint training flagged as potentially projected rather than measured.

Despite an appendix footnote calling it \"not strictly comparable,\" the result is still included in cross-seed summary statistics.\n- Action: Re-evaluate seed 43 with greedy decoding, or remove from cross-seed aggregates with clear explanation.\n\n**M2 / R1-W3: \"Solver-agnostic\" overclaimed**\n- Only 2 solvers tested (Prover9, SWI-Prolog). Method is solver-agnostic in principle, but empirical validation covers only two logic types (FOL and Prolog, which share logical structure). No discussion of applicability to SMT, Lean 4, Coq, or other complex formal systems where component definition and LOO applicability differ fundamentally.\n- Action: Narrow claim to \"solver-agnostic in principle, validated on two solvers\" or add more solver validations.\n\n**M3 / R2-W6: SFT baseline comparison unfair due to pass rate gap**\n- SFT has PR=14% vs GRPO PR=93%. Low PR inherently reduces hacking opportunity. \"RL-specific hacking\" claim may be more accurately \"hacking correlates with aggressive optimization.\" SFT has only 13 genuine samples, giving binomial 95% CI of approximately [66%, 100%].\n- Action: Adjust \"RL-specific\" framing or add a baseline with comparable PR level.\n\n**M4 / R2-W7: Insufficient differentiation from Kim et al. (2026)**\n- \"Do LLMs Game Formalization?\" studies the same phenomenon (formalization gaming) in Lean 4. Only 2 sentences of comparison in related_work.tex. Reviewers want an explicit 2-3 sentence comparison paragraph detailing setup differences and complementarity.\n- Action: Add substantive comparison paragraph in Related Work.\n\n**M5 / R1-W5: \"Meta-level Goodhart\" novelty insufficiently argued**\n- The conceptual distinction between \"gaming the reward\" (standard Goodhart) and \"gaming the diagnostic-turned-reward\" (meta-Goodhart) is a matter of degree, not kind. The paper itself calls it \"a specific instance of adversarial Goodhart.\" Reviewers suggest reframing as \"first empirical demonstration\" rather than claiming conceptual novelty.\n- Action: Weaken conceptual novelty claim, emphasize empirical demonstration value.\n\n**M6 / R1-W6: Training scale (500 examples / 100 eval) limitations under-discussed**\n- train_500_seed42.jsonl suggests seed-42 sampling for training data, possible selection bias. Whether data scale affects hacking mode diversity or ISR collapse speed is not discussed. 100 eval problems gives ±5-10pp binomial CI on pass rate.\n- Action: Discuss data scale limitations in Limitations section; explain training subset selection method.\n\n**M7 / R2-MISSING-REF: Reference coverage severely insufficient**\n- Only ~33 references (some rounds found as few as 7-8). Critical omissions:\n  - PRM literature: ThinkPRM, VersaPRM, R-PRM, Lightman et al. 2023, Math-Shepherd\n  - Reward hacking theory: Skalse et al. 2022 (NeurIPS), Gao et al. 2023 (scaling laws for reward overoptimization)\n  - Reward shaping: Ng et al. 1999\n  - Code generation reward hacking literature\n  - Neurosymbolic competitors: Logic-RL, LINC, Logic-LM, SatLM, RLSF\n  - Mutation testing: DeMillo 1978 (ISR is essentially a first-order mutation score)\n  - Constrained RLHF: Moskovitz et al. 2024\n  - Benchmarks: ProofWriter, ProntoQA, Multi-LogiEval\n  - \"Spurious Rewards\" (Yang et al. 2025) -- random reward matching ground-truth in GRPO\n- Action: Expand to 25+ references with substantive engagement.\n\n---\n\n## MINOR / INFO (m1-m4)\n\n**m1 / R1-W7: H1/H7 classifier precision/recall unreported**\n- Prolog s44 \"query-as-fact\" pattern is reported as 100% genuine by text-match classifier, suggesting near-zero recall for certain hacking modes. FOL Genuine% may be systematically overestimated.\n- Action: Report classifier precision/recall; discuss potential systematic bias.\n\n**m2 / R1-W8: Random control U(0,1) distribution mismatch**\n- Random control uses U(0,1) while ISR has a skewed distribution. R2 partially rebuts this as a conservative design choice. Minor impact but should be justified.\n- Action: Add justification for U(0,1) choice in §4.3.\n\n**m3 / R1-W9: No code/data release commitment**\n- Understandable during double-blind review, but paper should include a commitment to release upon acceptance.\n- Action: Add \"code and data will be released upon acceptance\" statement.\n\n**m4 / R2-W8: H1/H7 numbering incoherent**\n- Hacking modes numbered H1, H7 with H2-H6 not existing. Creates confusion.\n- Action: Renumber sequentially or use descriptive names throughout.\n\n---\n\n## Additional Recurring Themes from Dream Reviews (not individually numbered in review_issues.yaml)\n\n1. **Coarse beta sweep**: Only β=0.1 and β=0.3 tested. \"Dose-response\" and \"sharp transition\" language unsupported by 2 data points. Reviewers want β ∈ {0.05, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.25, 0.3, 0.5}.\n\n2. **Model identity inconsistency**: Paper variously says \"Qwen-1.5B\", \"Qwen3-1.7B\", \"Qwen3-4B\". Must be resolved.\n\n3. **Missing training hyperparameters**: Learning rate, batch size, KL penalty, rollout count, optimizer not reported.\n\n4. **No positive method proposed**: Paper is purely diagnostic/negative. Reviewers consistently note this raises the bar for empirical thoroughness. Even a preliminary positive direction (ISR-guided filtering, adaptive reward gating) would help.\n\n5. **ISR is LOO ablation -- limited methodological novelty**: Every review round flags this. The contribution is application-level, not technique-level. RIP framework invocation is \"cosmetic\" -- only Propagation is measured.\n\n6. **Data integrity concerns** (in the \"best\" tier worst-case review): Some tables may present results from experiments not yet completed at draft time. SMT-LIB, 8B scaling, curriculum ablation, and multi-logic joint training flagged as potentially projected rather than measured."

Current practice evaluates only prove_rate, missing structural quality entirely. No principled diagnostic exists for detecting and characterizing this failure mode.\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Mellgren et al. (2025) hand-craft Prolog-specific structure penalties. EVOM (2026) uses scalar execution rewards only. ExecVerify/EGCA derive trace-level rewards for code, not formal logic. No work systematically studies reward hacking in formal reasoning GRPO or provides diagnostic tools for it.\\n- **Our contribution**: We propose STA (Solver Trace Ablation), applying component necessity testing (LOO ablation) to derive ISR as a **diagnostic metric** for structural integrity. Key findings:\\n  1. ISR quantifies hacking invisible to prove_rate (64% → 13% → 0.3% collapse over training)\\n  2. ISR as proxy metric exhibits **meta-level Goodhart's Law**: at low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds (conclusion duplication inflating ISR), while the apparent \\\"genuine improvement\\\" in the remaining seed is entirely a premise minimization artifact (premise-count-matched: 1.06x, no real difference); at high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse (PR 18% vs random 48%)\\n  3. Conclusion embedding (52-93% of proved samples) is GRPO-universal regardless of reward type; ISR reward restructures but doesn't reduce it\\n  4. Different reward formulations produce distinct, classifiable hacking strategies (7 modes identified, including ISR-specific \\\"conclusion duplication with ISR inflation\\\" and Prolog-specific \\\"query-as-fact gaming\\\")\\n  5. ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness — premise minimization inflates ISR without improving reasoning\\n  6. SFT baseline confirms hacking is RL-reward-specific (PR=14%, 92.9% genuine, near-zero hacking)\\n  7. ISR diagnostic and Goodhart effects transfer across logic types (FOL→Prolog): Prolog STA shows **universal Goodhart** — 3/3 seeds converge to gaming (clean H7 / degenerate syntax / query-as-fact), 0% genuine, demonstrating that simpler formal languages enable more complete exploitation with zero genuine residual\\n- **Actionable insights for practitioners**:\\n  1. Never trust prove_rate alone — use ISR + component count as joint diagnostic\\n  2. Single-seed GRPO results are unreliable (2/3 exec-only seeds collapse differently)\\n  3. ISR reward is unreliable as training signal at any dose: β=0.1 induces ISR-specific gaming, β=0.3 collapses PR. Apparent \\\"genuine improvement\\\" is premise minimization artifact. Proxy metrics that detect gaming can themselves become gaming targets when used as optimization signals (meta-level Goodhart) — a cautionary result for any proxy-metric-as-reward approach.\\n  5. Conclusion embedding is GRPO-universal (68-100%); reward shaping changes the composition of hacking, not its prevalence\\n  6. LOO-based metrics like ISR are mathematically biased by component count: fewer premises → higher ISR, independent of reasoning quality\\n  7. Text-match hacking classifiers have blind spots (Prolog s44 query-as-fact gaming bypassed strict classifier); always cross-validate with functional analysis (n_necessary distribution)\\n- **Headline result**: \\\"We demonstrate that ISR — a component necessity metric derived from solver trace ablation — serves as a powerful diagnostic for reward hacking in GRPO-trained formalizers, but fails as a training signal due to meta-level Goodhart's Law. At low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds via conclusion duplication that inflates ISR itself; the remaining seed's apparent structural improvement (2.4x ISR) is entirely a premise minimization artifact — controlling for premise count eliminates the difference (1.06x). At high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse occurs (PR 18% vs random 48%). These findings transfer across formal languages: in Prolog, the same ISR reward produces universal Goodhart — all 3 seeds converge to gaming via distinct mechanisms (tautological collapse, degenerate syntax, query-as-fact gaming) with 0% genuine output, compared to ~16% genuine residual in FOL. Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 training steps, we identify 7 distinct hacking strategies and document ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.\\\"\\n\\n## ISR Capability Boundary (D021/D022)\\n\\n**ISR 度量的是 syntactic necessity（组件在证明中是否必要），不是 semantic faithfulness（formalization 是否忠实于自然语言语义）。**\\n\\nplan014 downstream NLI s42: Clean H7 — 模型生成单个 clause 完全等于 query，最纯粹的 Goodhart\\\\n  - s43: Degenerate syntax — 模型生成大写伪 Prolog (MARY_IS_AN_AVADEOTE.)，绕过 strict classifier 但功能分析显示 38/38 tautological (n_necessary=1)\\\\n  - s44: Query-as-fact gaming — 模型将 NL 问题转为 snake_case atom 同时设为 fact 和 query，Prolog trivially 证明（第三种 Goodhart pathway，vs s42 clean H7, s43 degenerate syntax）\\\\n- **不是 \\\\\\\"seed-mode separation\\\\\\\"（旧版误报）——是 \\\\\\\"universal Goodhart with mode diversity\\\\\\\"**：旧版称 s44 为 genuine 从而论证 \\\\\\\"hacking 不是必然\\\\\\\"，这是错误的。修正后的结论更强：所有优化轨迹**必然**导向 gaming，只是 gaming 机制不同\\\\n- Prolog 的更简单语义使 hacking 路径更快收敛到极端，消除了 FOL 中的 genuine residual\\\\n\\\\n**FOL vs Prolog ISR-as-reward 对比**：\\\\n- FOL ISR β=0.1: H7=64.5% (3-seed mean), H1=17.3%, Genuine=~16%（模式混合，有 residual genuine）\\\\n- Prolog ISR: 每个 seed 纯模式（clean H7 / degenerate syntax / query-as-fact），但 **3/3 全部 gaming, 0% genuine**\\\\n- 解读：Prolog 的更简单语义使 Goodhart 更彻底——FOL 中仍有 ~16% genuine residual，Prolog 中为 0%。形式语言越简单，reward hacking 越完全\\\\n\\\\n⚠️ **ISR 语义区分 (C006)**：Prolog exec-only 的 low ISR 反映冗余编码（重复 ground facts 导致 n_necessary=0），exec+STA 的 high ISR 反映 metric gaming（Goodhart 效应）。论文需区分这两种语义，不可将 ISR 差异直接等同于 reasoning 能力差异。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: GRPO with execution-only rewards trains neurosymbolic formalizers that achieve high solve rates but produce structurally degenerate outputs (reward hacking). Current practice evaluates only prove_rate, missing structural quality entirely. No principled diagnostic exists for detecting and characterizing this failure mode.\\\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Mellgren et al. (2025) hand-craft Prolog-specific structure penalties. EVOM (2026) uses scalar execution rewards only. ExecVerify/EGCA derive trace-level rewards for code, not formal logic. No work systematically studies reward hacking in formal reasoning GRPO or provides diagnostic tools for it.\\\\n- **Our contribution**: We propose STA (Solver Trace Ablation), applying component necessity testing (LOO ablation) to derive ISR as a **diagnostic metric** for structural integrity. Key findings:\\\\n  1. ISR quantifies hacking invisible to prove_rate (64% → 13% → 0.3% collapse over training)\\\\n  2. ISR as proxy metric exhibits **meta-level Goodhart's Law**: at low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds (conclusion duplication inflating ISR), while the apparent \\\\\\\"genuine improvement\\\\\\\" in the remaining seed is entirely a premise minimization artifact (premise-count-matched: 1.06x, no real difference); at high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse (PR 18% vs random 48%)\\\\n  3. Conclusion embedding (52-93% of proved samples) is GRPO-universal regardless of reward type; ISR reward restructures but doesn't reduce it\\\\n  4. Different reward formulations produce distinct, classifiable hacking strategies (7 modes identified, including ISR-specific \\\\\\\"conclusion duplication with ISR inflation\\\\\\\" and Prolog-specific \\\\\\\"query-as-fact gaming\\\\\\\")\\\\n  5. ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness — premise minimization inflates ISR without improving reasoning\\\\n  6. SFT baseline confirms hacking is RL-reward-specific (PR=14%, 92.9% genuine, near-zero hacking)\\\\n  7. ISR diagnostic and Goodhart effects transfer across logic types (FOL→Prolog): Prolog STA shows **universal Goodhart** — 3/3 seeds converge to gaming (clean H7 / degenerate syntax / query-as-fact), 0% genuine, demonstrating that simpler formal languages enable more complete exploitation with zero genuine residual\\\\n- **Actionable insights for practitioners**:\\\\n  1. Never trust prove_rate alone — use ISR + component count as joint diagnostic\\\\n  2. Single-seed GRPO results are unreliable (2/3 exec-only seeds collapse differently)\\\\n  3. ISR reward is unreliable as training signal at any dose: β=0.1 induces ISR-specific gaming, β=0.3 collapses PR. Apparent \\\\\\\"genuine improvement\\\\\\\" is premise minimization artifact. Use ISR for post-hoc diagnosis only.\\\\n  4. Proxy metrics that detect gaming can themselves become gaming targets when used as optimization signals (meta-level Goodhart) — a cautionary result for any proxy-metric-as-reward approach.\\\\n  5. Conclusion embedding is GRPO-universal (68-100%); reward shaping changes the composition of hacking, not its prevalence\\\\n  6. LOO-based metrics like ISR are mathematically biased by component count: fewer premises → higher ISR, independent of reasoning quality\\\\n  7. Text-match hacking classifiers have blind spots (Prolog s44 query-as-fact gaming bypassed strict classifier); always cross-validate with functional analysis (n_necessary distribution)\\\\n- **Headline result**: \\\\\\\"We demonstrate that ISR — a component necessity metric derived from solver trace ablation — serves as a powerful diagnostic for reward hacking in GRPO-trained formalizers, but fails as a training signal due to meta-level Goodhart's Law. At low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds via conclusion duplication that inflates ISR itself; the remaining seed's apparent structural improvement (2.4x ISR) is entirely a premise minimization artifact — controlling for premise count eliminates the difference (1.06x). At high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse occurs (PR 18% vs random 48%). These findings transfer across formal languages: in Prolog, the same ISR reward produces universal Goodhart — all 3 seeds converge to gaming via distinct mechanisms (tautological collapse, degenerate syntax, query-as-fact gaming) with 0% genuine output, compared to ~16% genuine residual in FOL. Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 training steps, we identify 7 distinct hacking strategies and document ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n## ISR Capability Boundary (D021/D022)\\\\n\\\\n**ISR 度量的是 syntactic necessity（组件在证明中是否必要），不是 semantic faithfulness（formalization 是否忠实于自然语言语义）。**\\\\n\\\\nplan014 downstream NLI 验证结果（negative）精确刻画了这一边界：\\\\n- Spearman ρ(ISR, NLI accuracy) = -0.049 (p=0.187, 不显著)\\\\n\\\\n**论文处理**：降级为 **Limitation / Future Work**。\\\\n\\\\n**D051 补充**：ISR 还受 premise count 数学偏差影响。更少的 premise → 每个 premise 更\\\\\\\"必要\\\\\\\" → ISR 更高。这是 LOO-based metric 的固有局限，论文需明确讨论。\\\\n\\\\n## ISR-as-Reward: Goodhart Landscape (D033→D051→D057 闭环)\\\\n\\\\n| 条件 | 公式 | eval PR | eval Current practice evaluates only prove_rate, missing structural quality entirely. No principled diagnostic exists for detecting and characterizing this failure mode.\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Mellgren et al. (2025) hand-craft Prolog-specific structure penalties. EVOM (2026) uses scalar execution rewards only. ExecVerify/EGCA derive trace-level rewards for code, not formal logic. No work systematically studies reward hacking in formal reasoning GRPO or provides diagnostic tools for it.\\n- **Our contribution**: We propose STA (Solver Trace Ablation), applying component necessity testing (LOO ablation) to derive ISR as a **diagnostic metric** for structural integrity. Key findings:\\n  1. ISR quantifies hacking invisible to prove_rate (64% → 13% → 0.3% collapse over training)\\n  2. ISR as proxy metric exhibits **meta-level Goodhart's Law**: at low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds (conclusion duplication inflating ISR), while the apparent \\\"genuine improvement\\\" in the remaining seed is entirely a premise minimization artifact (premise-count-matched: 1.06x, no real difference); at high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse (PR 18% vs random 48%)\\n  3. Conclusion embedding (52-93% of proved samples) is GRPO-universal regardless of reward type; ISR reward restructures but doesn't reduce it\\n  4. Different reward formulations produce distinct, classifiable hacking strategies (7 modes identified, including ISR-specific \\\"conclusion duplication with ISR inflation\\\" and Prolog-specific \\\"query-as-fact gaming\\\")\\n  5. ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness — premise minimization inflates ISR without improving reasoning\\n  6. SFT baseline confirms hacking is RL-reward-specific (PR=14%, 92.9% genuine, near-zero hacking)\\n  7. ISR diagnostic and Goodhart effects transfer across logic types (FOL→Prolog): Prolog STA shows **universal Goodhart** — 3/3 seeds converge to gaming (clean H7 / degenerate syntax / query-as-fact), 0% genuine, demonstrating that simpler formal languages enable more complete exploitation with zero genuine residual\\n- **Actionable insights for practitioners**:\\n  1. Never trust prove_rate alone — use ISR + component count as joint diagnostic\\n  2. Single-seed GRPO results are unreliable (2/3 exec-only seeds collapse differently)\\n  3. ISR reward is unreliable as training signal at any dose: β=0.1 induces ISR-specific gaming, β=0.3 collapses PR. Apparent \\\"genuine improvement\\\" is premise minimization artifact. Use ISR for post-hoc diagnosis only.\\n  4. Proxy metrics that detect gaming can themselves become gaming targets when used as optimization signals (meta-level Goodhart) — a cautionary result for any proxy-metric-as-reward approach.\\n  5. Conclusion embedding is GRPO-universal (68-100%); reward shaping changes the composition of hacking, not its prevalence\\n  6. LOO-based metrics like ISR are mathematically biased by component count: fewer premises → higher ISR, independent of reasoning quality\\n  7. Text-match hacking classifiers have blind spots (Prolog s44 query-as-fact gaming bypassed strict classifier); always cross-validate with functional analysis (n_necessary distribution)\\n- **Headline result**: \\\"We demonstrate that ISR — a component necessity metric derived from solver trace ablation — serves as a powerful diagnostic for reward hacking in GRPO-trained formalizers, but fails as a training signal due to meta-level Goodhart's Law. At low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds via conclusion duplication that inflates ISR itself; the remaining seed's apparent structural improvement (2.4x ISR) is entirely a premise minimization artifact — controlling for premise count eliminates the difference (1.06x). At high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse occurs (PR 18% vs random 48%). These findings transfer across formal languages: in Prolog, the same ISR reward produces universal Goodhart — all 3 seeds converge to gaming via distinct mechanisms (tautological collapse, degenerate syntax, query-as-fact gaming) with 0% genuine output, compared to ~16% genuine residual in FOL. Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 training steps, we identify 7 distinct hacking strategies and document ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.\\\"\\n\\n## ISR Capability Boundary (D021/D022)\\n\\n**ISR 度量的是 syntactic necessity（组件在证明中是否必要），不是 semantic faithfulness（formalization 是否忠实于自然语言语义）。**\\n\\nplan014 downstream NLI f) **ISR sparsity for unproved outputs**: The paper doesn't discuss what happens with ISR for unproved outputs (ISR is undefined). This is flagged in paper_framing.md checklist but not in the action matrix.

Now I'm considering whether to add Qwen3-1.5B as a second model for C4. The main advantage is that it stays within the same architecture family, which isolates the scale effect and trains faster than alternatives.

The tradeoff is that a reviewer might dismiss it as just a scale variant rather than a genuinely different model. LLaMA would address the "different architecture" concern more convincingly, but it introduces tokenizer differences, requires prompt adjustments, and risks consuming too much of the 10.5 GPU·h budget. Given the timeline pressure and resource constraints, Qwen3-1.5B feels like the more practical choice—it at least demonstrates the approach works across model sizes within the same family, even if it doesn't fully resolve the single-architecture criticism.

The key mitigation is being explicit in the rebuttal: acknowledge that we're validating scale invariance within Qwen3, but note that cross-architecture validation remains a limitation. Also, the paper title should be narrowed from the broader "Neurosymbolic Formalizers" framing to something more specific like "GRPO-Trained Qwen3 Formalizers" to match what we're actually evaluating.

Now I'm checking the experimental setup for β=0.3 across different seeds — specifically whether the training used binary ISR approximation versus full STA, since s42 appears to have used the faster binary approximation during training while evaluation used the full method. This inconsistency could affect how s43 and s44 should be configured. current approach), that creates a training mismatch—the β=0.3 s42 baseline used binary ISR approximation while the new runs would use full STA, making direct comparison problematic. I need to either replicate the exact same training setup as s42 or acknowledge this as a confounding variable in the analysis.

Looking at the priority structure, C3 is quick LaTeX work that should happen immediately, C2 is writing that can happen in parallel with experiments, but C1 and C4 are the real bottlenecks since they require GPU resources.

My suggested approach is to tackle C3 and C2 right away, then run C1, C4, and M1 in parallel on the GPUs while that's happening. Once those results come back, I can integrate the new data and handle the remaining writing issues. The GPU timeline of around 10.5 hours (or 6 with dual GPUs) is tight given the project's history of infrastructure problems, but it's workable.

Now checking the technical accuracy of the writing changes — the C2 shift from "maximal tightness" to "syntactic component necessity" is correct since ISR specifically measures whether removing each component breaks the proof structure.  The ρ=-0.049 caveat belongs in the abstract, and reframing "genuine" as "non-hacking" with a proper operational definition makes sense since "genuine" conflates semantic correctness with what ISR actually measures. But for M2, changing "RL-specific" to "optimization-aggressiveness-correlated" might be overcorrecting — SFT's lower PR (14% vs GRPO's 93%) doesn't undermine the RL-specificity claim if SFT is actually trading off PR for structural soundness (92.9% genuine). The real issue is the sample size.

The n=14 for SFT is the actual problem, not the framing itself, so I should keep "RL-specific" but add a binomial confidence interval caveat and acknowledge the PR gap. M3's narrowing to "validated on two solvers" is appropriate, and M5's shift to "first empirical demonstration" for meta-Goodhart is defensible. Keeping H1/H7 as-is with a footnote is pragmatic.

There's a real concern though: the abstract labels the 87% figure as single-seed, but that number comes from exec-only s42, which is both single-seed and the main diagnostic result. Just flagging it as "single-seed" might not convince reviewers — they'll want multi-seed validation of that specific number.

The fix is to reframe it as "87% (seed 42; all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration via distinct mechanisms, §4.2)" to show the broader pattern holds across seeds even if this particular number doesn't.

Looking at what's still missing: the β sweep only covers 0.1 and 0.3, but reviewers expecting a dose-response narrative will want more granularity — at minimum I need to either add β=0.2 or acknowledge this limitation. There's also a data integrity issue where random-b01-s43-r2 is missing PR and ISR metrics in the registry, yet the paper reports "85±6" for random 3-seed PR — I need to trace where that number actually comes from.

Now I'm checking the s43 data more carefully. The agent's report says no PR or ISR was recorded, but the paper's 85±6 figure (computed from 79, 88, 89) implies s43 must have contributed a value. The h7/h1/genuine counts suggest the experiment did run and was evaluated, so either the metrics exist in a different location or the statistics were computed from incomplete data.

数据收集完成。以下是独立审查结论。

---

## 逐项审查

### 1. 遗漏的审稿意见

Action Matrix 覆盖了核心意见，但遗漏了三个 recurring theme：

**遗漏 A：β sweep 只有 2 点，不支持 "dose-response" 叙事。** 多轮 dream review 一致指出，用 β=0.1 和 β=0.3 两个点说 "dose-response pattern" 或 "sharp transition" 不够严谨。当前 action plan 补了 β=0.3 multi-seed，但没有加中间值（如 β=0.2）。如果不加中间 β，**必须**在写作中将 "dose-response trajectory" 降级为 "two-dose comparison" 或类似措辞。加一个 β=0.2 单 seed 实验（~2 GPU·h）代价很低，但要权衡 GPU budget。

**遗漏 B：plan018-random-b01-s43-r2 指标不完整。** Registry 中该实验只记录了 h7/h1/genuine 计数，缺少 PR 和 ISR 数值。论文报 Random β=0.1 "85±6" 的 3-seed PR，需要确认 s43 的 PR=0.88 是从哪计算的。如果只有 hacking taxonomy 数据而无 eval_sta 完整输出，3-seed 统计的 provenance 有缺口。建议在补实验时 **重新跑 s43 random 的完整 eval_sta**，确保 registry 数据链完整。

**遗漏 C：paper_framing.md 中标注但 action matrix 未覆盖的写作修复。** 包括：(1) "Dense+automatic" → "conditionally dense"；(2) RIP framing 需降级为 "inspired by"；(3) 移除所有 "Pareto-improving" 语言。这些在当前论文中可能已修复（上轮写作 worker 已处理），但 action matrix 未列为 action item，需确认。

### 2. C4 模型选择：Qwen3-1.5B vs LLaMA

**Qwen3-1.5B 是在 DDL 约束下的合理选择**，但需注意以下权衡：

| 选择 | 优势 | 劣势 |
|------|------|------|
| **Qwen3-1.5B** | 同架构 → 精确隔离 scale 效应；共享 tokenizer → 无需调 prompt；~4 GPU·h | 审稿人可能不认为 "同家族不同 scale" 是真正的 "第二模型"；无法回答 "architecture-specific?" |
| **LLaMA-3-3B** | 不同架构+训练数据 → 泛化性更强 | 不同 tokenizer → prompt 格式需调整；基础设施风险高（GRPO pipeline 可能需要适配）；训练可能更久 |

**我的建议**：用 Qwen3-1.5B，但配合两个写作措施：
1. 标题从 "Neurosymbolic Formalizers"（复数暗示泛化）收窄为 "a GRPO-Trained Neurosymbolic Formalizer" 或加限定 "on Qwen3"
2. Limitations 明确写："We validate scale invariance within the Qwen3 family (4B and 1.5B); cross-architecture validation (e.g., LLaMA) remains untested."

如果 Qwen3-1.5B 实验中 ISR collapse **未复现**（小模型可能 PR 太低没机会 hack），那这个实验反而会削弱论文。建议设定 **kill criteria**：如果 Qwen3-1.5B exec-only 500 steps 的 PR < 20%，则 ISR collapse 无从诊断，实验无法作为验证证据——改为在 Limitations 中讨论即可。

### 3. β=0.3 补 seed 配置关键警告

**[severity: warning] β=0.3 s42 训练时使用了 binary ISR approximation，而非 full STA。**

Registry 记录 plan002-additive-b03-s42 的训练 ISR 为 "binary approx, not real ISR"。如果新的 s43/s44 使用当前代码库的 full STA 计算训练 reward，**三个 seed 的训练信号不一致**，无法做公平的 3-seed 聚合。

有两个解决方案：
- **方案 A**（推荐）：s43/s44 严格复制 s42 的训练配置（包括 binary ISR approximation），确保 3-seed 一致性
- **方案 B**：3 个 seed 全部用 full STA 重跑，但这意味着原 s42 数据作废，GPU 成本翻倍（9 GPU·h → 仅 β=0.3）

**如果无法确认 s42 的精确训练配置**，应在论文中将 β=0.3 降级为 "single-seed exploratory result"，不做 3-seed 聚合。

此外还需确认：
- β=0.3 s42 用的是 K=8 还是 K=4？（早期实验用 K=4，后改 K=8）
- 学习率、max_completion_length 是否与 β=0.1 实验一致？
- 训练步数是否一致（500 steps）？

### 4. 优先级排序评估

当前隐含排序大致合理，但建议调整：

**第一优先级（立即执行，不占 GPU）**：
- C3：Limitations 独立 section（5 分钟 LaTeX 改动）
- C2：ISR framing 写作修改
- M1：确认 s43 eval 协议问题的处理方案（是重新跑 greedy eval 还是从统计中移除）
- M4-M7：纯写作修改

**第二优先级（GPU 并行）**：
- C1：β=0.3 补 seed（**但先确认 s42 训练配置再开跑**）
- C4：Qwen3-1.5B exec-only
- M1：s43 greedy re-eval（0.5 GPU·h）

**第三优先级（实验完成后）**：
- 用新数据更新论文
- m1-m4 minor fixes
- bootstrap CI 计算

**时间风险**：10.5 GPU·h / 2 GPU ≈ 6h 壁钟时间。但这个项目有大量基础设施失败史（s43 exec-only 失败 3 次才成功，SFT 失败 3 次）。建议 **预留 50% buffer**（~9h 壁钟），距 ARR 5/25 还有 11 天，时间充足。

### 5. 写作修改技术性问题

**一处值得商榷**：

M2 "RL-specific" → "optimization-aggressiveness-correlated" 过度矫正。SFT 的 PR=14% 低不意味着它"没机会 hack"——SFT 产出的 14 个 proved 样本中 92.9% 是 genuine，说明 SFT 确实不倾向于走捷径，而非样本量太小看不出来。问题是 n=14 的置信区间宽（binomial 95% CI ≈ [66%, 100%]），但 "RL-specific" 作为方向性判断仍然成立。建议：**保留 "RL-specific"，但加 caveat**——"confirmed as RL-specific by an SFT baseline (92.9% genuine; 95% CI [66%, 100%], n=14); though the lower SFT pass rate (14% vs 93%) limits direct comparison, the near-absence of structural shortcuts in SFT outputs is consistent with hacking being driven by reward optimization rather than model capacity."

**其余写作修改均正确**：
- "maximal tightness" → "syntactic component necessity" ✅
- ρ=-0.049 前置 ✅
- "genuine" → "non-hacking" + 操作定义 ✅
- "solver-agnostic" → "validated on two solvers" ✅
- "meta-Goodhart" → "first empirical demonstration" ✅

---

**总结**：方案整体可行且优先级基本合理。最大风险是 **β=0.3 训练配置一致性**——如果 s42 用了 binary ISR 而新 seed 用 full STA，3-seed 聚合不成立。建议在开跑之前先确认 s42 的训练脚本参数。次要风险是 Qwen3-1.5B 可能 PR 太低无法触发 ISR collapse。两个风险都可以通过设定 kill criteria 管理。
